not possible to invest directly in an index.

The latest rotation is not the first attempt at a change in market leadership this year.
The more important question is what that shift represents: a fundamental reassessment
of the AI trade or another step toward broader, more mature market leadership?
Recent Q3 Technology earnings results have sharpened that debate. Investors are
increasingly looking beyond the scale of AI spending to companies’ ability to translate
that investment into durable growth and attractive returns. The result may be greater

dispersion not only between Growth and Value, but also among AI leaders as investors
become more discerning about which companies can justify the scale of their
investments.
Investment Implications
We continue to look for potential
missing opportunities and favor
diversification across and within asset
classes in a multi-asset portfolio.
In particular, we see potential
opportunities to broaden exposure
beyond AI leaders toward areas
where earnings, capital spending and

productivity trends are improving,
including Value, Small- and Mid-caps,
and select non-U.S. markets, particularly
EMs and Japan. As market leadership
evolves, maintaining diversified
exposures and periodically rebalancing
portfolios can help investors participate
in a broader set of possible return
drivers without becoming overly
dependent on any single theme.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But greater selectivity does not necessarily signal the end of the AI investment cycle.
YoY growth rates may become harder to maintain after two years of rapid acceleration
as the comparison base rises, but absolute levels of AI capital spending remain elevated.
What matters more is whether spending translates into strong productivity and earnings
growth ahead and whether AI demand continues to support investment. October’s

volatility may therefore reflect less a retreat from the AI theme and more a recalibration
of valuations and expected returns, as other areas of the market begin to contribute
more meaningfully to market performance.
Several developments ahead will help determine whether that rotation has staying
power. One important test is whether broader participation can persist if pressure on AI
stocks continues. Continued strength outside the AI trade would support the case that

investors are reallocating capital rather than broadly reducing risk. The path of monetary
policy will also matter as investors assess where borrowing costs may be headed. The
Federal Reserve’s not-so-hawkish hold in October offered some reassurance, but the
path ahead remains dependent on the evolving balance between inflation and growth.
Any signs of resolution of geopolitical tensions remain an important swing factor
particularly as recent flashpoints in the Middle East have pressured energy prices higher

again. Results of the midterm elections should also provide greater visibility into the
policy landscape for the next few years. While the market does not need every clearing
event to resolve favorably, greater clarity around these uncertainties could help narrow
the range of potential outcomes facing investors.
For investors, October’s sudden change in leadership reinforces the importance of
diversification. Leadership can shift quickly, leaving portfolios that become overly

dependent on a single theme, sector or style particularly vulnerable when it does. We
remain constructive on U.S. Equities and believe that the market may be transitioning
more toward a mid-cycle phase in which returns become broader but also more
measured compared to the sharp acceleration earlier in the cycle. That argues not for
abandoning the market’s long-term winners, but for complementing them with exposure
to additional sources of returns across style, size, sector and region.

### Page 6

6 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Reorienting around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction to Capital Goods
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
For all the questions (and recent market weakness) hanging over the AI trade, from
monetization and cash burn to financing risk and overcapacity, a measurable aspect is
the buildout itself. Data center construction is lifting construction spending and capital

goods orders, while companies are adding and reviving capacity to meet AI-related
demand. Still, planning delays, access to power, labor scarcity, local politics and the cost
of capital remain meaningful constraints on the buildout’s pace or profitability.
Corporate Plans. Take Caterpillar as one example that has resumed production of a
10-megawatt natural gas turbine discontinued back in 2022. A 175-year old glassmaker,
Corning, is leaning into optical connectivity as AI clusters require faster connections of

optical fiber cabling between servers. Caterpillar and Corning are just two companies
among others pivoting to supply data centers.
Construction to Capital Goods. The AI reorientation is evident in construction
spending as well, where data center construction has outpaced every other major
residential and nonresidential category. Over the last year AI-related construction on data
centers has surged more than 60% while spending on residential, lodging, commercial

or office has declined notably (Exhibit 5A). Data center construction is running at an $85
billion (B) annualized rate this year, versus roughly $49B a year earlier, and now accounts
for 11% of private nonresidential construction, up from just 2% in 2022 according to
the Census Bureau. Similarly, demand for artificial intelligence business equipment and
transportation equipment drove an increase in durable goods orders in September on the
back of new orders for U.S.-manufactured capital goods, suggesting business spending

on equipment remained robust in Q3. Another factor, see Exhibit 5B, where critical
components such as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers continue to
face long lead times that affect delivery timelines and ultimate profitability for new data
center development.
These signposts reinforce our view that we are only in the middle innings of the AI
investment cycle. From an investment perspective, huge demand for data centers, power
systems, networking infrastructure, specialized chips and other equipment continues

to strain supply, creating both bottlenecks and potential opportunities for companies
powering the buildout.
Exhibit 5: Work Zone: Construction Spending and Input Supply Lead Times.
A)  Data Centers and Power are Bright Spots in Construction Spending. B) AI-Critical Equipment Lead Times.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500/Market-cap Weighted Index is a market-capitalization-weighted index that is widely regarded as the best single gauge of large-cap U.S. equities. The index includes 500 leading

companies and covers approximately 80% of available market capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index includes the same constituents as the capitalization weighted S&P 500, but each company in the S&P 500 EWI is allocated a fixed weight - or 0.2% of the
index total at each quarterly rebalance.
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index is a broad-based, market-capitalization-weighted benchmark that measures the performance of the U.S. investment-grade, U.S. dollar-denominated,

fixed-rate taxable bond market
Bloomberg Commodity Index is a broadly diversified financial benchmark that tracks the price movements of physical commodities through futures contracts.
MSCI Emerging Market Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets (EM) countries.
United Nations Food and Agriculture Food Price Index is a measure of the monthly change in international prices of a basket of food commodities.
Philadelphia Semiconductor Index is a market-cap-weighted index focusing on 30 leading semiconductor firms, offering insights into the tech market.

Investing involves risk, including the possible loss of principal. Past performance is no guarantee of future results.
Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase

or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and
investment advisory services, including the type of advice and assistance provided, the fees charged, and the rights and obligations of the parties. It is important to understand the differences,
particularly when determining which service or services to select. For more information about these services and their differences, speak with your Merrill financial advisor.

oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 364: FX Daily_ A dollar downturn lacks the rate ingredient

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Daily_ A dollar downturn lacks the rate ingredient.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The post-midterm dollar correction requires a worsening of USD
rate diﬀerentials to turn into a downturn. But as oil prices fall,
other central banks are facing similar or even larger dovish
repricing than the Fed. Data is key now, but indications are that
they won’t turn USD-negative soon. Today, expect a hike in the
UK and rate holds in Norway and Mexico

Francesco Pesole, Frantisek Taborsky and Chris Turner
We don't see much room for the dollar drop to extend
USD: Front-end rates should prevent a
bigger decline
The Democratic sweep at the midterms delivered the negative
dollar impact we had expected. But as we argued before the vote,
we didn’t see a case for a bigger USD downturn. In our view, rates
need to start moving against the dollar to justify a greater
adjustment, unless idiosyncratic US asset underperformance fuels
the move. But fears that a Democratic sweep would hit tech stocks
didn’t materialise. Instead, a potential relief that the midterm risk

### Page 2

event is out of the way, alongside plummeting oil prices, is fuelling
an equity rally.
The key hurdle for a bigger USD downturn is that lower oil prices are
not unequivocally USD-negative. Let’s look at yesterday’s moves in
rates. As crude dropped 8%, two-year USD swap rates fell 5bp,
broadly in line with CAD, AUD and SEK swaps. EUR, GBP and NZD
2yr swap rates, however, fell more, by around 8-10bp. To us, that is
the most compelling argument against chasing the dollar much

lower. Poorer US data and/or a more dovish Fed remain the
necessary conditions for front-end USD rates to fall more than
peers, and by extension for a broader USD downturn.
Instead, data has remained broadly supportive of Fed tightening
expectations. Yesterday, the ISM services index was a tad softer
than expected but remains well into expansionary territory. The
employment subindex rose to 51.4, the highest since February.
Paired with strong ADP payrolls at 86k, that’s likely to reinforce

expectations for a robust payroll print tomorrow – consensus is 65k,
but the whisper number has risen to 70k.
Today’s data highlights are the Challenger jobs cut ﬁgures for
October and 3Q unit labour costs. We favour a stabilisation around
101.0-101.50 in DXY in the coming days, rather than another break
lower.
Francesco Pesole
Elsewhere, Banxico announces its rate decision later today. The
peso has been the worst-performing EM currency over the last two

months after Banxico said it would not be following the Fed with a
rate hike in September. In fact, some are looking for Banxico to heap
more fuel on the peso’s ﬁre with a rate cut today. Such a move
could see USD/MXN push to 19. Either way, Banxico has slashed the
peso’s rate protection to ultra-low levels, and the peso remains very
vulnerable to global shocks.
Chris Turner
EUR: Knot pitches his hawkish agenda

### Page 3

Klaas Knot delivered a few remarks yesterday after the Netherlands
oﬃcially backed his candidacy for the ECB presidency. He was
ﬁrmly hawkish, claiming second-round inﬂation eﬀects will
probably pick up, and that the ECB will need to respond with higher
rates to small shocks that will lift inﬂation. We’ll wait to hear from
Pablo Hernández de Cos, who is the other frontrunner for the job,
and a long-standing dove. An appointment of Knot would be a

EUR-positive event given his hawkish views, in our view.
Meanwhile, eurozone inﬂation accelerated from 3.8% to 4.0% as
expected in October. That brings the ECB closer to a December hike,
as argued here by our macro team, even if core inﬂation remained
anchored at 2.5%.
As mentioned in the USD section, the EUR:USD two-year swap rate
gap widened against the euro yesterday as oil prices tumbled. A
similar dynamic prevented EUR/USD from rallying in the summer

when oil fell back to $70. Now, at least one more ECB hike – likely in
December – looks like a done deal, providing a stronger ﬂoor for
eurozone rate expectations. Still, this rate component, combined
with lingering French risk, leaves us unexcited about a break higher
in EUR/USD. We continue to see little upside potential beyond
1.130, our year-end target.
Francesco Pesole
GBP: BoE to resume tightening today
We expect the Bank of England to hike rates today by 25bp to 4.0%

(full preview here), in line with market expectations and consensus.
A roughly 25% rise in household energy bills in January now looks
increasingly likely, and we expect headline inﬂation to peak just
above 4% early next year. That has often been indicated as the
pain threshold for the BoE. Still, evidence of second-round
inﬂationary eﬀects remains limited.
We expect a 7-2 vote for a hike, and don’t see the BoE committing
to further tightening at this stage. A follow-up move in February

remains our base case, but policymakers should keep the
emphasis ﬁrmly on incoming evidence of energy pass-through into

would therefore constitute a hawkish surprise.
We think today’s hike can validate the pound’s recent gains, for
now. However, a February hike is fully priced in, and the bar for a
hawkish surprise lifting the pound seems fairly high. We don’t see
immediate rebound potential for EUR/GBP, which is still dampened
by the French eﬀect, but GBP/USD is facing a less supportive short-
term rate diﬀerential, as the GBP curve shows greater dovish

ﬂexibility when oil prices drop. We currently expect stabilisation
around 0.840 in EUR/GBP, while Cable may see its upside capped at
1.350 in the near term.
Francesco Pesole
NOK: Norges Bank on hold today
Norges Bank should keep rates on hold today. Having been one of
the quickest central banks to respond to fresh inﬂationary risks and
given rates are already at an elevated 4.5%, the bar for further
tightening remains fairly high. Incidentally, September CPI ﬁgures

were a bit softer than expected, with underlying inﬂation staying at
3.0% and headline accelerating less than expected to 3.4%.
These are still elevated ﬁgures, so don’t expect Norges Bank to close
the door to rate hikes yet. The threat of further action, if needed, will
remain for some time, but we do not expect any further tightening
in Norway, in line with Norges Bank’s September rate projections.
Markets are pricing in just under 50% chances of another hike by

March.
The krone is facing some headwinds due to the big drop in oil
prices. However, it’s protected by much less hawkish pricing than
other central banks, therefore facing less dovish pressure on energy
price corrections. Yesterday, the NOK two-year swap rate dropped
only 2bp. While this oil move can take EUR/NOK back to 10.50, a
return to $80-85/bll may be needed for a return to the late
September 10.90 levels. We see the pair ending the year in the

10.55-10.65 area.

In Sweden, following yesterday’s 25bp rate hike, inﬂation ﬁgures for
October came in well below consensus. CPIF slowed more than
expected to 1.1%, with the core measure excluding energy
plummeting to 0.1% YoY from 0.5%. These were preliminary
ﬁgures, and expectations are anyway of a pickup to above 2.0% in
both CPIF and the ex-energy measure in 2027, in our view, justifying
another hike in February. That likely explains the contained SEK
reaction to the print. Still, these ﬁgures support our view of a

consolidation around 11.00 in EUR/SEK as opposed to another big
leg lower.
Francesco Pesole
CEE: Lower energy prices overshadow
NBP and CNB meetings
In Poland, the NBP left interest rates unchanged at 3.75%
yesterday, as expected, with the accompanying statement
presenting a mixed picture. On one hand, the forecast included a
signiﬁcant upward revision to the inﬂation outlook; on the other, the
statement noted lower core inﬂation in October and an anticipated

economic slowdown in the third quarter of 2026. Notably, the NBP’s
data cut-oﬀ point was likely two weeks ago. Consequently, the
projections do not account for the downside surprise in October’s
inﬂation ﬁgures, while they do reﬂect record-high energy prices,
which, from today’s perspective, appear somewhat more
favourable. Governor Adam Glapiński’s press conference today is
expected to maintain a dovish tone, and we do not anticipate the
NBP’s ﬁrst rate hike until the ﬁrst quarter of 2027.

In the Czech Republic, we expect the CNB to hike rates by 25bp to
4.00%, the second such increase since the onset of the US-Iran
conﬂict. The CNB remains the most hawkish central bank in the CEE
region, and its new forecast is expected to reinforce this stance. The
updated projections will likely point to higher inﬂation, interest
rates, and wages, alongside a hawkish external environment, while
the GDP growth forecast will likely be revised downward. The CNB

has scaled back its forward guidance. We do not expect ﬁrm
commitments regarding future policy steps today, but rather an

Given the rally in interest rates over the past few days, the threshold
for a "hawkish" surprise is lower. The market is currently pricing in

roughly 80bp of tightening, down from a peak of 125bp. That said,
the CNB has generally delivered less hawkishness this year than the

CEE currencies and pushing rates lower. A dovish tone from the NBP

## Document 368: Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Jan Hatzius 
+1(212)902-0394 | jan.hatzius@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
Global Views: Downside Growth Risks
26 October 2026 | 6:45AM EDT
1. So far in 2026, global growth has been remarkably unremarkable.  Both the world 
as a whole and the G3 economies are growing in line with our estimates of potential 
GDP .  Our interpretation is that fiscal easing and the positive impulse from the AI 
boom have offset the negative impulse from the war in Iran and the lingering drag 
from higher US tariffs.

2. Our standing global GDP forecasts project more of the same in 2027, as lower 
energy prices offset fading tailwinds from fiscal policy and AI.  But the balance of 
risks is on the downside.  The most immediate concern is that, far from subsiding, 
energy prices have again climbed sharply on fears of renewed Middle East escalation.  
The prices of gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, and European/Asian natural gas are now near 
or even above their March/April highs, with likely negative effects on consumers and 
energy-importing economies more broadly.

### Page 2

3. Although we don’t expect significant fiscal policy changes in 2027, the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—have 
started to show up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates.  The moves have 
been mostly orderly, but the increase since early 2026 averages a sizable 100bp across 
DM yield curves.  If sustained, we estimate that this increase will subtract a cumulative 
0.6% from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment.  
(Only part of the drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect bond 
yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.)

Exhibit 2: Oil Price Risks Are Skewed to the Upside

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 3

4. The growth impulse from the AI boom was set to peak in late 2026 or early 2027 even 
before concerns around the sustainability of the AI investment boom intensified in 
recent weeks.  Although AI investment is still rising rapidly, we expect the increase as a 
share of GDP to slow over the next year.  Alongside the fading boost from last year’s 
increase in US depreciation allowances, this will weigh on capital spending growth, 
especially in the US.  AI has also boosted US consumer spending growth via a positive 
wealth effect worth an estimated 0.4pp (or 0.25% of GDP) over the past year.  This 
impact is likely to decline over the next year, gradually if stocks perform in line with our 
strategists’ reasonably upbeat expectations but more sharply if AI-related stocks 
continue to decline.

### Page 4

5. Our own base case remains that AI investment will ultimately pay off in terms of higher 
productivity and economywide profits.  However, one key channel for this payoff is labor 
market disruption, which has negative near-term macroeconomic effects.  And while DM 
labor markets still look solid in aggregate, we are starting to see clearer signs that the AI 
impact is spreading beyond the technology sector.  Across nonmanufacturing industries, 
the share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment.

6. The Fed’s recent signals—explicit from Williams/Jefferson and implicit from Warsh—
effectively rule out a hike on October 28.  But the FOMC still looks very likely to deliver 
another 25bp hike to 4-4.25% on December 9 because spot inflation remains too high.  
And while we continue to expect significant disinflation next year on the back of 
reduced impulses from tariffs, energy prices, and AI-related prices, the risks to our 
forecast are on the upside, most obviously in energy.  Higher inflation could result in

another hike in March, but either way we see renewed cuts in late 2027/early 2028.

7. Like the Fed, the ECB will likely hold rates steady next week and deliver a final hike (to 
2.75%) at the December meeting.  However, the turbulence in the French bond market 
poses somewhat greater downside risks to that call than in the US.  The turbulence has 
tightened financial conditions to a degree that is likely to shave ¼-½pp from annualized 
growth in coming quarters.  And if it intensifies into December, markets might perceive a 
hike as signaling insufficient focus by the ECB on the risk of Euro area fragmentation.  
While the ECB could address this issue by coupling a hike with renewed balance sheet 
expansion, the hurdle for such a move is high, in part because purchases of French 
bonds would inevitably raise concerns about moral hazard with respect to the needed 
fiscal adjustment.

### Page 6

November 5.  Consistent with our view that the UK economy is stronger than widely 
perceived, GDP growth has surprised on the upside this year and the unemployment rate 
declined to 4.7% in August on a 3-month average basis (with the volatile 1-month rate 
plunging to 4.1%).  Rising inflation and the looming household energy price reset in 
January imply a significant risk of further moves, although market pricing of 4+ hikes 
(including November) still looks excessive.  We also expect hikes in Canada and New 
Zealand this week and in Australia next week.

9. Slightly faster than expected growth in Q3 has pushed up our 2026 GDP forecast for 
China to 4.6%.  But most activity indicators are much weaker.  Both property activity and 
fixed asset investment are contracting year-on-year, and while nominal retail sales 
growth picked up to +1.1% in September, large retailers (whose sales are relatively 
better measured) are still posting substantial year-on-year declines.  With domestic 
demand rising slowly at best, sustained GDP growth in the official 4.5-5% target range 
will require ongoing steep increases in the trade surplus.  But the surplus is already at 1% 
of global GDP , and further increases will eat more noticeably into output and 
employment abroad.  Ultimately, China’s trading partners may respond by imposing 
trade barriers that weigh on Chinese exports and GDP , although the measures taken so 
far have been limited.

Exhibit 8: UK Growth Forecasts Have Drifted Higher

Source: Bloomberg, Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   6
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 7

10. Our strongest global view is that central banks will deliver fewer hikes than currently 
priced.  In addition, our rates strategists flag underweight positioning, diminished 
technical risks, and risk asset pressures as reasons why the recent rally might extend.  
The market’s narrow breadth makes our equity strategists wary of momentum factor 
exposure, but they still expect the S&P 500 to resume its climb after the midterm 
election, as earnings continue to grow solidly.  Our credit strategists remain cautious on 
IG technology bonds but see scope for some near-term reversal in HY given the scale of 
the recent selloff.  Our FX strategists expect the Japanese yen to act as a hedge against 
further AI jitters, in part because such jitters would shrink US-Japan interest rate 
differentials.  And our commodity strategists recommend targeted exposures to oil 
products and European natural gas to hedge near-term escalation risks alongside 
longer-term exposures to copper, gold, and select power markets to benefit from 
structural supply/demand imbalances. 
Jan Hatzius

## Document 373: India_ Degrees of rupee defence (2026-10-16)

TAKING STOCK

The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has adopted a 
multi-layered defence strategy to support the 
rupee this year.

This has entailed a combination of outright 
intervention in spot/ forwards, regulatory 
action, liquidity steps, administrative measures 
and moves to curb import -related dollar 
demand.

The rupee has appreciated by 1.8% against the 
dollar in the past five days since recent 
measures took effect, paring YTD depreciation 
to 5.5% . High oil prices, weakness in capital 
inflows, a firm dollar , tariffs/ geopolitical 
worries, energy , and risk -off sentiments have 
kept the INR under pressure since last year.

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026

ECHOES OF 2013 WITH FEW 
DIFFERENCES

Comparisons with 2013 are apt, though 
the backdrop differs.  As for the 
catalysts, markets reacted to the US 
Fed’s taper announcement in 2013, 
which pushed US yields higher  and led 
global investors to reduce exposure to 
vulnerable EM economies. As a result, 
capital inflows weakened, exposing 
funding mismatch es for countries, 
including India, that were running wide 
current account deficits.

In 2026, markets face the spectre of 
elevated oil prices, geopolitical 
uncertainty, global competition for 
capital, a firm dollar ( DXY appreciated 
+3.8% YTD) , and US inflation/ deficits 
which have settled at levels much higher 
than pre -pandemic years. This has 
pushed up UST yields, and the US Fed 
kicked off its rate-hike cycle in Sep26.

Against this backdrop, the RBI is 
pursuing the same objective , i.e., to 
ensure rupee stability, but the nature of 
the battle differs. The defence strategy 
has shifted from ‘crisis management’ 
mode in 2013 , due to weaker domestic 
macroeconomic balances (a wide current 
account gap, high inflation, a lower 
reserve buffer, and wide fiscal deficits) , 
to a ‘terms-of-trade shock and impact of 
a firm dollar’ in 2026.

Flashpoints are  nonetheless similar, 
with reliance on portfolio financing , 
leaving the external account vulnerable 
to shifts in global risk sentiment. 
Portfolio outflows have emerged as the 
primary transmission channel through 
which external shocks have translated 
into INR depreciation pressure , 
compounded by high energy prices and a 
firm dollar.

FX PLAYBOOK

While the response framework is similar 
between 2013 and this year, the broad 
sequence and nature of macroprudential 
measures differ. This year’s multi-
layered rupee defence toolkit has 
included:

-Direct FX defence  by way of 
intervention in the spot and forwards 
market. As of Aug26, the net forward 
dollar liabilities ballooned to $200bn, 
rising by ~$63bn vs . the month before . 
Recent sell/buy FX swaps are expected 
to lower this stock in the coming months.

-Attracting dollar inflows  by way of 
special swap facilities for FCNR deposits, 
and offshore borrowings , apart from 
liberalising taxes around debt inflows,

### Page 3

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

amongst others. We discussed this here, 
here, here and here.

Taking a proactive stance, a portion of 
existing FX reserves could be earmarked 
against forward liabilities (from maturing 
FCNR deposits) , helping to mitigate 
concerns that deposit maturities or debt 
repayments could trigger a sharp
increase in dollar ⁠demand down the line.

-Domestic liquidity management. Bonds, 
to some degree, are paying the price for 
the authorities' efforts to defend the 
rupee against the additional headwind of 
weak global conditions.  INR liquidity 
surged on the back of the swap windows, 
prompting the RBI to undertake open 
market operations, VRRR auctions, sell -
buy swaps, among others. While these 
have been effective in absorbing 
balances, bond yields have drifted higher. 
10Y yields are at three-year highs and are 
expected to climb further  (DBSf: 7.40 -
7.50% by year-end).

-Moderate FX demand and speculation . 
Earlier in the year, the RBI had 
introduced change s to the banks’ net 
open position framework, and thereafter 
partially rolled back a few curbs. 
Prudential measures on FX exposures 
and hedging -related measures were 
announced in Oct26, which include d the 
introduction of a daily cash reserve with 
the RBI equal to 20% of the rupee 
equivalent of the notional value for INR -
linked derivative contracts exceeding 
$2mn used to hedge current account 
transactions, referred to as the Foreign 
Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR) . 
Subsequent clarifications provided 
operational details on where the FERR 
would be maintained and that it would be 
subject to audits.

Since 12 Oct, the RBI has been providing 
dollars via designated banks to the three 
state-owned oil -marketing companies 
(Indian Oil, BPCL, and HPCL) to meet the 
their daily dollar requirements, thereby 
diverting related spot-market demand.

On hedging-related matters, the limit for 
undertaking foreign exchange derivative 
transactions without proving an 
underlying commercial exposure was 
cut to $5mn from $100mn across all ADs 
and exchange -traded currency 
derivatives. Restrictions on Rebooking , 
i.e., a uthorized dealers are prohibited 
from allowing customers to rebook 
cancelled rupee -linked derivative 
contracts (though rollovers upon 
maturity remain permitted). Lastly, 
banks must obtain written confirmation 
from users that the same underlying 
exposure has not been hedged with 
another dealer.  Data from CCI , cited by

### Page 4

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Bloomberg, pointed to importers’ 
forward dollar purchases rising 44% on 
the year to a monthly average of $64bn.

-Current account management . Efforts 
to contain structural FX outflows by way 
of higher duties and taxes on gold 
imports discourage non -essential 
purchases and production incentives to 
improve export competitiveness.

IMPACT

To assess whether the playbook has 
worked, we observe:

a) Rupee has enjoyed a brief respite. 
Despite oil prices at $105/bbl at the time 
of writing and elevated global yields, 
USDINR fell from near 96.80 to wards 
95.00, owing to measures announced in 
Oct26 following the central bank’s 25bp 
hike. Beyond temporary relief, global 
catalysts are likely to be back to the fore 
and weigh on the currency (see chart);

b) Sharp inflows under the swap 
facilities pushed the foreign reserves 
stock to a record high, providing 
policymakers sufficient buffer to 
navigate future shocks from a position of 
strength. A strong interventionist 
response has since led to a softening in 
the overall stock (see chart).

c) Macroprudential measures have led to 
distortions in other parts of the market . 
For instance, strong swap -related 
inflows had inorganically pushed 
onshore INR liquidity up sharply, 
necessitating absorption measures. 
Similarly, NOP restrictions and the more 
recent tighter requirements on hedging 
activity and counterparty transactions  
had some after -effects. Forward points 
have surged this week , with financial 
institutions pointing to relative illiquidity 
in the currency forwards market  on 
volumes and activity basis, and fewer 
avenues to deal with dollar supply.

### Page 5

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

d) Swap-related windows are a timely 
support for the country’s balance of 
payments position. From expectations of 
a third consecutive year of BOP deficit in 
FY27, the math has now swung to a 
potential surplus, even as current 
account worries remain. With energy 
prices settling north of $100/bbl, 
keeping the monthly trade deficit near 
$30bn, our FY27 current account deficit 
forecast of 1.1% of GDP faces  upside 
risks of around 0.3-0.5% of GDP. We note 
the s harp jump in ECB outflows in S ep, 
though see that as a temporary overshot 
of leverage-FCNR activity.

WHAT LIES AHEAD

Authorities are likely not done yet and 
remain ready to take further action, as 
required. RBI Governor Malhotra 
reiterated the authorities’ serious intent 
of stabilising the currency in a recent 
interview (Moneycontrol, 13 Oct), 
emphasising that “The RBI is determined 
to do whatever is necessary to ensure 
orderly movement in the foreign 
exchange market,”. He also highlighted 
that recent steps were not tantamount to 
capital control measures that were 
aimed at ensuring that forex markets 
serve genuine hedging needs rather than 
speculative bets.  Market participants 
opine that FX volumes and interest are 
likely to be subdued in a few pockets on 
the back of the recent measures, 
consequently stabilizing the rupee.

Unlike in 2013, when policy tightening 
was a part of the initial response, the 
2026 strategy has prioritised targeted 
FX and regulatory measures.  The 
sequencing suggests a deliberate effort 
to distinguish actions aimed at 
preserving currency stability from those 
intended to achieve domestic monetary 
objectives, with rates remaining a 
potential, though not immediate, tool in 
the defence framework.

However, global developments have 
altered that dynamic . Rising US yields 
and a gradual increase in the US Fed 
Funds rate (DBS baseline: a total of 75bp 
hikes) have compressed IN -US spreads. 
The recent sell -off in INR benchmark

### Page 6

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

bonds has effectively widened Gsec -
UST spreads across maturities, in 
contrast to the compression witnessed in 
regional high-yielders.

The latest announcements may not 
fully quell speculation over further 
policy action.

Upcoming measures might include -

-Markets anticipate relief on the long -
term capital gains tax structure on 
equities to draw in longer-duration 
inflows. This measure will boost inflows 
if it coincides with other factors that 
make domestic equities attractive for 
thematic investing , as global risk -free 
rates moderate.

-Street expectations also include steps 
to boost inward dollar repatriation by 
exporters to boost domestic dollar 
supply.

-Wider policy rate corridor akin to 2013.

-Policy rate response. While this might 
not be a panacea, it is a likely necessary 
condition. Raising the neutral rate might 
have mixed success in attracting capital 
market inflows ; though rate-sensitive 
flows are likely to get a fillip as investors 
seek attractive rate differentials in the 
region.

An adequate real rate cushion would 
also align with the evolving domestic 
inflation realities, which are expected to 
accelerate to 6% in the Oct-Dec quarter 
and remain above 5% well into 2027.

Given fluid global developments, we 
outline three scenarios on the rate path 
ahead. Our baseline assumption  is for 
75bp hikes in this cycle  (including Oct), 
spread between the Dece mber and 
February 2027 meetings. We don’t rule 
out an intermeeting rate hike before the 
December meeting, contingent on 
rupee movements.

Under the second scenario , s hould 
depreciation pressures on the rupee 
return, the 50bp hike might be brought 
forward to December , with another in 
February, taking cumulative hikes in this 
cycle to 100bp. The t hird scenario  is a 
more aggressive 150bp worth of hikes in 
this cycle, with 50bp front -loaded in 
December, and the rest spilling into 2027. 
Factors that  will determine whether 
these scenarios materialise are the 
resumption and scale of rupee 
depreciation, oil price direction, US Fed 
Funds rate increases (DBSf: total 75bps) 
and other risk-off events.

Path ahead

Despite these measures, we see limited 
room for sustained INR appreciation on 
grounds of manufacturing/ trade 
competitiveness. A challenging flows 
portfolio flows outlook leaves the d oor 
open to further gradual rupee 
depreciation subject to global 
developments, with high sensitivity to oil 
price movements and risk sentiments . A 
strong rate response will help introduce  
favourable rate differentials and reduce 
currency volatility.

► 美國9月非農數據公布後一度令 10年期公債
殖利率出現明顯回落 。然觀察最新 ISM服務
業之價 格分 項 指 數 較上月 出現反彈 ， 並創
2022年7月以來 最高水準 ，反映運輸 及原物
料成本上升 ，顯示通膨壓力仍可能持續向其
他產業傳導 ，公債殖利率因而再度反彈並持
續於高檔徘迴 ，多數債券市場在利率高檔震
盪環境下亦呈現跌多漲少現象。
► 受法國債務問題與西班牙提前大選等歐洲 政
治與經濟消息影響 ，歐元走勢疲弱 。儘管近
期日圓於日銀9月升息後不升反貶，然市場亦
擔憂持續走貶可能再度引發干預 ，令短線日
圓呈現區間震盪走勢。
► G7宣布透過國際能源總署協調釋放 1億桶柴
油及原油等緊急 儲備，令油價短線回落 ，然
隨中東衝突前景未明 ，供應不穩之變數短期
難解，多空因素交錯恐使油價於高檔進行反
覆波動。

► 美國經濟仍具韌性 ，惟通膨壓力猶存 。觀察
美國9月ISM服務業指數表現 ，內需活動維持
穩健，企業投入成本壓力亦有所升溫；另一
方面，受地緣政治因素影響 ，美國柴油價格
大幅攀升 ，而柴油為卡車運輸 、農業及製造
業的重要燃料 ，若價格持續維持高檔 ，恐進
一步增添通膨壓力 。考量聯準會偏鷹立場 ，
且當前通膨降溫進程不如預期 ，年底前仍保
有升息空間。
► 美國債務總額突破40兆美元，預估2026年公
眾持有聯邦債務占 GDP的比率將達 101%，
財政可持續性風險升溫 。與此同時 ，AI基礎

建設舉債熱潮 ，疊加中東局勢動盪帶來的通
膨不確定性 ，迫使投資人重新定價長天期債
券風險 ，要求更高的風險溢酬 。隨實質殖利
率上行，美債10年期殖利率揚升並持續高懸
於5%水準之上，30年期長債殖利率亦同步站
上近20年來高點 。隨長天期債券利率續揚 ，
殖利率曲線趨陡峭 ，債券配置宜先避開長天
期部位，聚焦中短天期債券鎖利機會。

### Page 6

6
投資焦點
► 距離美國期中選舉投票僅剩不到1個月，觀察
Polymarket賭盤預估數據顯示 ，眾議院選情
較為明朗，民主黨有超過9成的機率控制眾議
院。但參議院選情則尚有變數 ，今年以來兩
黨均互有領先 ，雖然數據顯示民主黨掌控參
議院機率呈現上揚 ，然隨時序進入選前最後
衝刺期 ，參議院能否亦由民主黨掌控仍待觀
察。故就選前而言 ，由於不同政黨的政策傾
向差異 ，潛在受惠產業亦有不同 ，選舉結果
的不確定性恐令投資人不易大舉押注股市。
► 另一方面 ，油價高檔徘迴 ，導致年底前通 膨
擔憂仍存 ，加上AI企業發債需求成長 、美國
財政體質惡化等 因素共同發酵 ，推動近期 美
債殖利率逐步攀高 ，並令股市預期本益 比壓
力延續 ，進而限縮了投資人短期對美股的追
價動能，短期指數恐維持震盪輪動格局 。可
留意抗波動 、體質佳且具價值優勢之標的 ，
待期中選舉結束與3Q26財報展開後，不確定
性減退將有助資金回歸長線AI題材。
殖利率攀升令美股本益比面臨壓力參議院期中選舉選情仍具變數
期中選前擾動與殖利率走高，美股短期恐仍震盪輪動

### Page 7

7
投資焦點日股
► 9月日本銀行決議升息 1碼，明確表示潛在
CPI已接近2%，未來也將繼續調高利率 ，政
策重點開始轉向防範通膨向上偏離目標 。然
因有2位委員反對升息，加上總裁植田和男會
後談話偏向中性 ，令市場解讀此次升息未如
預期鷹派，美日利差不易快速縮窄，故9月會
議後日元反而呈現利多出盡的回貶反應。
► 觀察今年以來日股面臨短線日圓升值的狀態
並未出現顯著走弱 態勢。一方面反映企業治
理改革推動股東報酬 提升已成為 支撐股價的
重要結構性因素 。另一方面 ，升息代表日本
經濟已具備一定的健康程度 ，貨幣政策正常
化將不致對需求帶來衝擊 。因此 ，隨BoJ延
續升息方向 ，將擴大銀行淨利差 ，改善其獲
利結構，並提升金融資產收益 。儘管近期整
體日股預期獲利成長走勢略顯停滯 ，然日本
銀行股 在總 體經 濟 與貨幣 政策環 境的 支持
下，獲利成長有望逆勢攀升 ，料將成為後續
資金關注重點。
日圓升值對日股影響似乎有限，關注銀行股獲利走強BoJ升息方向延續，惟其步伐仍取決於經濟與物價

BoJ政策保持正常化路線，日本銀行股有望持續受惠

### Page 8

8
股市
 公債殖利率走高雖可能壓抑 股債市場近期 動能，然而觀察美股第三季獲利年增 率有望仍為雙位數成
長，或支撐股市表現 。市場持續關注AI資本支出是否帶來獲利成長 ，短期內評價面壓力將增添市場
動盪，可分散配置於不同國家與產業 ，降低市場波動影響 。總結上述，地緣衝突、殖利率偏高及評
價面壓力等紛擾 帶來干擾，可布局防禦 股增強抗震力 。長期而言，科技及AI半導體發展趨勢不變且
基本面佳，可逢拉回時分批布局。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，加上全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍
工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融及醫療保健產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，且最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示 ，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升息空間 ，立場
偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨 發展。殖利率偏高環境建議以中短天期債券投資為主 ，可逢利率走
升時布局進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸

引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期的鷹派，日圓震盪。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期紛擾難解，偏高的油價可能對通膨及實體經濟帶來影響。
 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

^New 
Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds . Near-
term event risks and high  earnings expectations prompt us to trim  equities to a 
Core holding.  The US and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive  income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain  Overweight gold . Prices are likely to grind higher as near -term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high  earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead.  In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran . These  concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly f or the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite t he current c hallenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop . The US economic outlook remains well supported , and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

Fig. 1 Our quant model score has fallen to levels 
associated with flat-to-modestly-negative equity returns  
 Fig. 2 Negative gold-USD correlation has held back

Our quantitative stock-bond model  Gold and USD correlations over the last 15 years

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered  Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long -term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects  this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar  by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and A xJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect the m to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers.  Our positive view on both markets’  
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India.  Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI -related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification . In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic idea s also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months  – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment -grade (IG) 
and high -yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3 -7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance.  We close our EU  bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 27) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective  tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream , while  rotating into alternatives  
helps reduce  equity beta exposure . We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub -
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4 ,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability):  We maintain the probability at 30% a s AI-driven investment growth  continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.

Policy rates watch  
We expect the Fed to hike twice more by Q2 2027, then 
ease in H2 2027 , as AI-led expansion continues.  The US 
economy remains firmly in expansion. In Q3, annualised GDP 
growth accelerated to 3.4 %, led by AI investments and 
consumption. In October, the composite Purchasing 
Managers’ Index (PMI) remained strong at 57.6. Since our last 
monthly report, the consensus estimate for 2026 GDP growth 
has been nudged up to 2. 3% from 2.1%, and the 2027 
estimate has risen to 2.2%. That matches the Fed’s upgraded 
growth projection of 2.3% for 2026 , but remains below the 
Fed’s 2.4% estimate for 2027. AI investment, fiscal support 
and household wealth are the main growth drivers, although 
consumer activity lags business spending. Higher bond yields 
and elevated oil prices are headwinds, but recession risk s 
look contained. Our core scenario remains a ‘soft landing’, at 
45%, with a meaningful 30% chance of a ‘no-landing’ upside. 
Rising gasoline and diesel prices pushed US core personal 
consumption expenditures ( PCE) inflation to 0.3% m/m and 
3.1% y/y, matching estimates. Consensus sees c ore PCE 
inflation slowing from 3.2% in Q4 2026 to 2. 4% by Q4 2027 
as energy and tariff effects fade. Underlying price momentum 
is softening, and recent PCE methodology revisions have 
lowered core inflation. However, fuel costs passed on to wider 
consumers and AI-driven chip shortages remain upside risks. 
In October, the Fed held rates  at 4.0% , after hiking in 
September for the first time since 2023. We expect the Fed to 
hike rates by 25bps in December and 25bps more in H1 2027 
due to elevated near-term energy inflation. It is likely to follow 
with a 25bps cut to 4.25% in Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This aligns with the Fed’s projected policy path, although it 
implies a more dovish trajectory than money markets, which 
are pricing another 56bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
Political uncertainty after the mid-term election is a key risk.

6 Macro overview – at a glance

Rajat Bhattacharya 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8 
ECB to hike once more  in December, then pause . The 
Euro area recovery is firming. GDP grew 1.2% y/y in Q2 2026, 
up from 0.6% in Q1, and the manufacturing PMI stayed robust 
at 52.4 in October. Consensus 2026 growth has been raised 
to 1.0% from 0.9%, and 2027 is unchanged at 1.3%. The main 
supports are resilient exports and German infrastructure and 
defence spending. However, cautious consumers and soft 
private investment limit the recovery, and energy costs are 
squeezing household incomes and business margins. 
Recession is not the central expectation. French fiscal stress 
and sustained high energy costs remain the key risks. 
Higher energy prices pushed Euro area headline inflation up 
to 3.8% y/y in September from 3.2% y/y in August, while core 
inflation rose to 2.5%  y/y. Consensus expects headline and 
core inflation to ease to 2% y/y and 2.4% y/y by Q4 2027, 
respectively. Encouragingly, energy has had limited impact on 
core prices, and longer -term inflation expectations remain 
stable at around 2.1%. Together, these should allow inflation 
to moderate in 2027. The main risk is that energy , and 
especially gas, prices stay high for longer than expected. 
The ECB held its deposit rate at 2.5% in October after raising 
it by 50bps this year . Inflation data continue to favour an 
additional hike in December. We expect an ECB rate hold in 
H1 2027, followed by a cut by Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This policy path is more dovish than money markets , which 
are pricing more than 60bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
China to provide targeted support to defend growth floor. 
China's growth momentum recovered in September. Both the 
manufacturing PMI (50.1) and the non -manufacturing PMI 
(50.2) moved back into expansion. The construction PMI rose 
above 50 for the first time this year as infrastructure spending 
started to take effect. Exports grew 36.3% y/y, pushing the 
trade surplus to a record USD  133.5bn. We expect growth in 
2026 to meet the lower end of the 4.5-5.0% target. Growth is 
increasingly bifurcated, though , with  technology firms and 
exporters doing well, while household demand stays weak. 
Prices are rising again, but only narrowly. Consumer inflation 
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, and producer inflation 
surged to 5% y/y, its highest since 2022, driven by energy and 
metals prices and concentrated in upstream sectors. With 
domestic demand weak and a high comparison base next 
year, the boost from energy prices is likely to fade in 2027. 
Policy is in ‘targeted easing’ mode. We expect a modest fiscal 
package to defend the growth floor rather than a large 
stimulus. The PBoC cut a key  rate by 25bps to 1.5% to fund 
‘Six Networks’ infrastructure projects and raised its relending 
quotas. A new interest subsidy on first -home mortgages 
provides limited support to demand. We expect no policy rate 
cut this year. There is still room to speed up fiscal spending, 
including by using CNY 500bn of unused local -government 
debt quota. If the PBoC switches to an overnight reverse repo 
rate as its policy rate, that would imply a de facto rate cut as 
the overnight rate is lower than the 7-day official policy rate. 
BoJ to hike at a  quarterly pace  until Q2 2027.  Japan's 
economy remains solid. Wages are growing above 4% , and 
the labour market is tight. The main headwind is high energy 
prices, which is slowing growth. Fiscal spending gives some 
support: the FY27 draft budget includes ambitious spending 
plans, alongside a proposed two-year cut to the consumption 
tax on food and a new five -year defence programme. 
However, this extra spending raises questions about fiscal 
sustainability. The BoJ unveiled plans to triple the pace of ETF 
sales from January 2027, potentially funding the tax cut. 
Price pressures now look firmly established. Headline and 
core inflation rose above 2% for the first time this year, and 
core-core inflation (ex-fresh food and energy) jumped to 2.4% 
and is expected to rise towards 3%  in Q1 2027 . Breakeven 
inflation rates have stayed above 2% most of this year. Real 
interest rates remain negative, hurting savers and pensioners. 
The BoJ held its policy rate at 1.25% in October, after raising 
it in September, only three months after its previous hike. This 
points to an accelerated quarterly hiking pace. We expect the 
quarterly pace of hikes to continue, taking the rate to 2% by 
June 2027. Money markets have caught up with our hawkish 
view in recent months. The BoJ noted for the first time that 
underlying inflation has reached its 2% target, marking a shift 
from creating inflation to containing it. Politics could slow the 
hiking cycle  in H2 2027. Two government -appointed board 
members voted against the September hike, and the two most 
hawkish members are due to leave in July 2027.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

Our view

Foundation: Bond yields remain near multi-year highs, offering an attractive entry point. We view fixed income as a Core holding 
(ie, a Neutral allocation) in our multi-asset portfolios. Within the asset class, we maintain an Overweight stance on EM USD 
government bonds, given their attractive risk-reward. We hold an  Underweight view on Developed Market ( DM) government 
bonds due to the tail risks associated with term premia. 
The Fed’s decision to hold rates at 3.75-4.00% in October amid still-elevated energy prices suggests that every Fed meeting 
from here is ‘live’, with a rate change possible on incoming data . With US inflation still running hot and the labour market tight, 
we expect two more 25bps  hikes (one by year-end and one in H1 2027), bringing the Fed’s target range for the federal funds 
rate to 4.25-4.5%. We continue to favour the 3 -7-year part of the  US government bond yield curve as the best hedge against 
any risk-off move driven by AI-related growth fears. As inflation pressures ease in H2 2027, we see room for the Fed to consider 
a rate cut, which should pull nominal yields lower. Solid corporate fundamentals and supportive technicals should keep spreads 
tight, but there is limited room for them to tighten further. We still favour EM USD over local-currency (LCY) government bonds. 
Opportunistic ideas: We are bullish on US TIPS, AAA-rated CLOs, broad US HYs and Australian dollar (AUD) corporate bonds.

DM rates – Underweight 
We remain Underweight DM government bonds. In the US, 
government bond yields have retraced meaningfully after a 
sharp bear steepening through September and early October. 
We expect real yield s to stay elevated over the next six 
months as AI capex and productivity growth continue to drive 
above-trend economic growth. We expect two more 25bps 
rate hikes by June 2027.  
Over the next few months, we expect US government bond 
yields to remain rangebound. The near-term path hinges on 
five factors: 1) the 3 November US mid-term election, where 
a ‘Democratic sweep’ would likely be bond -supportive by 
constraining US President Trump’s fiscal spending agenda , 
while a ‘Republican sweep’ would likely weigh on bonds as 
markets price in further fiscal expansion and a higher term 
premium; 2) Fed communication at upcoming policy meetings; 
3) the US Treasury’s quarterly refunding announcement on 4 
November, where a ny signal of reduced long -end issuance  
would likely compress long-end yields; 4) softening auction 
demand and repatriation by foreign investors, which add 
uncertainty around demand for US government bonds; and 5) 
the f luid geopolitical situation in the Middle East , which is 
keeping oil prices elevated, with a risk of further upside. 
Going into H2 2027, we expect moderating oil prices and the 
fading inflationary impact of tariffs to give the Fed room to 
consider rate cuts. The US 30-year yield, however, is likely to 
stay elevated as fiscal deficit remains substantial next year.  
We expect a final ECB hike to 2.75% by December 2026, then 
a pause in H1 2027, followed by a 25bps cut in Q3 2027. In 
Japan, we expect quarterly pace of BoJ hikes, with one hike 
that takes the policy rate to 1.50% by December 2026 and two 
more in H1 2027, reaching 2.00% before pausing at mid-year.

### Page 10

Global Market Outlook 10 
DM corporates – Core holding 
We maintain a Neutral allocation to both DM IG and DM HY 
corporate bonds, with a tactical bias to add exposure on 
episodic weakness, as carry continues to dominate total 
returns across DM credit. Credit spreads have widened from 
their lows but remain close to historical tights, while yields are 
still attractive, reinforcing a carry-led stance. This tight-spread 
regime is anchored by robust issuer fundamentals, strong 
profitability and resilient balance sheets, despite a visible 
moderation in rating upgrades over the past quarter.  
Technical supply dynamics are reshaping sector dispersion,  
especially in the IG space, where heavy AI-related issuance 
is weighing on the long end of the curve. AI -driven supply is 
surpassing USD 2 50bn in 2026 and mak ing up a growing 
share of US IG gross issuance, but there are growing signs 
that hyperscalers are turning to private markets for fundin g, 
which should help ease public bond market supply pressures.  
We remain selective at the long end  of the yield curve and  
favour a duration of  3-7 years, where risk -adjusted carry 
remains most durable. Issuance-driven concessions in 
sectors with strong capex cycles (notably technology, capital 
goods and basic industries) present opportunities for investors 
who can stomach near-term volatility. 
Fig. 12 DM IG spreads have widened from their lows, 
but remain near historically tight levels 
Bloomberg Global Aggregate Corp Index, option-adjusted 
spread (OAS)

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
DM HY spreads  have widened to roughly a six-month high. 
Persistent doubts about whether hyperscalers can generate 
adequate returns on massive AI capex investments triggered 
a broad risk asset sell-off. The CCC tier has become the focal 
point of genuine credit concern, with yields hovering near the 
high-15% range and spreads near a four-year high. Market 
focus has shifted to the funding pressures facing AI-related 
neocloud companies. We view this  as idiosyncratic for now 
and see a limited impact on the broader HY universe.  The 
underlying credit picture is mixed. Leverage is elevated and 
interest coverage is thin, but the broad market has not yet 
shown signs of systemic stress. The US HY d efault rate  
remains below trend in 2026. 
HY offers an attractive income proposition, with all-in yields of 
over 8%. Its shorter-duration profile, combined with its lower 
sensitivity to changes in the term premium and rate volatility, 
make it an attractive sleeve in the current phase of the cycle.  
We remain attentive to evolving credit rating migration trends 
and sector-specific supply pressures, particularly as upgrades 
slow and new issuance clusters around key themes. However, 
healthy issuer fu ndamentals and stable credit metrics  
continue to support it as a Core holding allocation. 
Fig. 13 DM HY spreads are off the tights 
Bloomberg Global HY Aggregate Corporate Index, OAS

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM government bonds – Overweight 
USD/Core holding for LCY 
We are Overweight EM USD government bonds and retain 
EM LCY government bonds as a Core holding, reflecting a 
balanced perspective across both regional performance and 
currency dynamics. In EM USD sovereign debt, the recent 
spread widening offered an attractive entry point to gain 
exposure to areas benefiting from resilient global growth and 
stable external conditions. Technicals remain favourable, with 
supply being front-loaded and investor positioning remaining 
relatively light compared with historical levels. 
Many EM central banks having tightened earlier and more 
aggressively post -pandemic, preserving credibility and 
maintaining positive real rates. External balances have 
improved, reserve buffers have increased and greater 
reliance on domestic funding reduces external vulnerability. 
Lula’s surprise win in Brazil’s presidential election raises 
political risk. The country’s fiscal trajectory under the incoming 
administration is a key risk to monitor. Fiscal slippage could  
trigger a broader repricing of LATAM risk. In Asia, higher-rated 
issuers continue to offer a valuable buffer during bouts of 
global rate volatility, while select frontier exposures in EMEA 
remain more sensitive to liquidity shifts and changes in risk 
appetite. Index composition also shapes resilience: the EM 
USD benchmark is less exposed to oil price shocks , as 
countries that are not oil importers account for a larger share 
of the benchmark than in  the LCY benchmark. We expect 
returns to be primarily driven by carry rather than further 
spread compression.

Global Market Outlook 11 
Fig. 14 EM USD government bonds offer a yield pick-up 
of nearly 3ppt over DM peers 
Bloomberg EM USD Government Bond Index, yield to worst

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM LCY debt faces additional headwinds, especially as 
elevated US real yields and an appreciating USD dampen 
demand from foreign investors and reduce the comparative 
attractiveness of broad -based duration strategies. However, 
real yields remain comparatively supportive in several 
markets, and current valuations signal selective opportunities, 
especially given the restrictive monetary policy stances 
prevalent across many EM economies. Accordingly, EM LCY 
bonds retain their Core holding status, with portfolio emphasis 
placed on country selection and measured duration exposure, 
reflecting the differentiated currency dynamics at play. 
Key risks to both  the USD and LCY segments include a 
potential escalation of the  Middle East conflict , higher oil 
prices, renewed hawkishness from the Fed that could support 
further USD strength and weather-related disruptions, such as 
the effects of El Niño . Climate risks could prolong inflation 
pressures and hinder disinflation in vulnerable EM regions. 
Asia USD bonds – Core holding 
We retain a Neutral allocation to Asia USD bonds, an asset 
class that continues to reward investors with attractive 
nominal yields, favourable supply -demand technicals and 
robust credit fundamentals. Healthy corporate cash flows, low 
leverage and a high share of sovereign-linked issuers lend the 
segment a defensive quality relative to broader credit. 
The resilience has been evident year to date (YTD). Asia USD 
bonds have outperformed  both DM government and DM IG 
corporate bonds  on a total -return basis. We expect this 
relative strength to persist. The combination of carry and 
credit quality (over 60% of issues are sovereign or sovereign-
related) anchors our conviction in the asset class, even as we 
maintain a Neutral stance.

Bond opportunistic views 
Bullish US HY bonds 
We are opportunistically bullish on US HY bonds. We expect 
HY corporate earnings and cash flows to remain solid and 
leverage to remain stable. US HY bonds’  shorter-duration 
profile offers lower sensitivity to interest-rate volatility, which 
is an attractive characteristic in an environment of elevated 
term premia. 
Bullish US inflation-protected bonds (TIPS) 
Real yields remain elevated versus historical standards , 
driven by rate hike expectations. Inflation concerns persist as 
energy prices remain subject to upside risks. We believe TIPS 
offer protection against near-term inflation uncertainty.   
Bullish AUD corporate bonds 
The threshold for further RBA hikes has risen, as higher 
energy prices weigh on the growth outlook and complicate the 
policy trade-off. The transmission channel matters too, where 
higher long -end yields could feed through to the property 
market, tightening financial conditions without the RBA having 
to act, casting further doubt on the case for additional hikes. 
Technicals have improved slightly as higher yields slow new 
supply. Supply remains a tail risk, though. The AUD bond

market is much smaller than the USD market, especially at the 
long end of the curve, where demand for duration is thinner 
and the term premium is most sensitive to supply. 
Bullish AAA-rated CLOs 
Concerns about spillovers from p rivate credit are mounting 
amid deteriorating covenant protections in parts of the 
leveraged credit market. However, we believe AAA-rated 
CLOs backed by solid asset portfolios and substantial credit 
enhancements underneath senior tranches should help 
dampen credit risk and volatility. We see a compelling case 
for floating-rate and shorter -duration alternatives as portfolio 
diversifiers amid elevated term premia.  
Close utilities corporate hybrids 
AI capex is set to keep rising, but funding it has become 
harder. Tighter financial conditions now coincide with growing 
competition for capital. Utilities' credit fundamentals remain 
strong. Even so, we close our utilities corporate hybrids idea 
to lock in a gain of 0.5% as a risk-management step.

### Page 12

Global Market Outlook 12

Our view

We downgrade global equities from Overweight to a Core allocation as we expect higher volatility in the near term. We 
expect this volatility to arise as equity market leadership broadens during the transition to a mid-cycle bull market. Equity returns 
will likely moderate from the 15-20% annualised range of the last few years but remain positive. We remain Overweight US and 
AxJ equities, which are expected to deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 among the major regions. 
Within AxJ, we remain Overweight Taiwan, given structural AI-driven growth, and upgrade India to Overweight, for diversified 
non-AI growth exposure. South Korea is a Core holding, with attractive valuations but extreme volatility . China is also a Core 
holding, with attractive valuations but lacking a positive catalyst. We are Underweight ASEAN, which has a weaker growth profile. 
We maintain Japan as a Core holding as nominal growth supports broad-based earnings revisions. We also maintain a Core 
allocation to Europe ex-UK, which is enjoying an earnings recovery. We remain Underweight UK equities, given the market’s 
limited exposure to growth sectors.

8 Equity – at a glance

Sundeep Gantori, CFA, CAIA 
Chief Investment Officer, Equities 
Fook Hien Yap
